----- Row 16 -----
raw_knowledge_statement: \textsc{QLoRA} introduces multiple innovations designed to reduce memory use without sacrificing performance: (1) \textbf{4-bit NormalFloat}, an information theoretically optimal quantization data type for normally distributed data that yields better empirical results than 4-bit Integers and 4-bit Floats. (2) \textbf{Double Quantization}, a method that quantizes the quantization constants, saving an average of about 0.37 bits per parameter (approximately 3 GB for a 65B model). (3) \textbf{Paged Optimizers}, using NVIDIA unified memory to avoid the gradient checkpointing memory spikes that occur when processing a mini-batch with a long sequence length.
questions: - "What quantization data type introduced by \textsc{QLoRA} is described as information theoretically optimal for normally distributed data?", Answer: "4-bit NormalFloat"

- "Which \textsc{QLoRA} innovation quantizes the quantization constants?", Answer: "Double Quantization"

----- Row 35 -----
raw_knowledge_statement: For example, quantizing a 32-bit Floating Point (FP32) tensor into a Int8 tensor with range $[-127, 127]$:

\begin{equation}
    \mathbf{X}^{ \text{Int8}} = \text{round}\left( \frac{127}{{\text{absmax}}(\mathbf{X}^{{\text{FP32}}})}\mathbf{X}^{{\text{FP32}}} \right) = \text{round}(c^{\text{FP32}}\cdot \mathbf{X}^{{\text{FP32}}}),
\end{equation}

where $c$ is the {\em quantization constant} or {\em quantization scale}.
questions: - What range is used for the Int8 tensor in the FP32-to-Int8 quantization example?  
  **Answer:** $[-127, 127]$

- In the quantization equation, what is $c$ called?  
  **Answer:** quantization constant

    \text{dequant}(c^{\text{FP32}}, \mathbf{X}^{\text{Int8}}) = \frac{\mathbf{X}^{\text{Int8}}}{c^{\text{FP32}}} = \mathbf{X}^{\text{FP32}}
\end{equation}

raw_knowledge_statement: Given a projection ${\mathbf{X} \mathbf{W} = \mathbf{Y}}$ with $\mathbf{X}\in  \mathbb{R}^{b\times h}$, $\mathbf{W}\in  \mathbb{R}^{h\times o}$ LoRA computes:

\begin{equation}
    \mathbf{Y} = \mathbf{X}\mathbf{W} + s\mathbf{X}\mathbf{L}_1\mathbf{L}_2,
\end{equation}

where $\mathbf{L}_1\in  \mathbb{R}^{h\times r}$ and $\mathbf{L}_2\in  \mathbb{R}^{r\times o}$, and $s$ is a scalar.

\begin{equation}
q_i  = \frac{1}{2}\left( Q_X\left(\frac{i}{2^k+1}\right) + Q_X\left(\frac{i+1}{2^k+1}\right)\right),
\end{equation}

raw_knowledge_statement: \paragraph{Double Quantization{}}

raw_knowledge_statement: \paragraph{\textsc{QLoRA}.} Using the components described above, we define \textsc{QLoRA} for a single linear layer in the quantized base model with a single LoRA adapter as follows:

\begin{equation}
    \mathbf{Y}^{\text{BF16}} =  \mathbf{X}^{\text{BF16}} \text{doubleDequant}(c_1^{\text{FP32}}, c_2^{\text{k-bit}}, \mathbf{W}^{\text{NF4}}) + \mathbf{X}^{\text{BF16}}\mathbf{L}^{\text{BF16}}_1\mathbf{L}^{\text{BF16}}_2,
\end{equation}

where doubleDequant$(\cdot)$ is defined as:

\begin{equation}
    \text{doubleDequant}(c_1^{\text{FP32}}, c_2^{\text{k-bit}}, \mathbf{W}^{\text{k-bit}}) = \text{dequant}(\text{dequant}(c_1^{\text{FP32}}, c_2^{\text{k-bit}}), \mathbf{W}^{\text{4bit}}) = \mathbf{W}^{\text{BF16}},
\end{equation}

  Answer: "\mathbf{W}^{\text{BF16}}"

  Answer: "\mathbf{X}^{\text{BF16}}\mathbf{L}^{\text{BF16}}_1\mathbf{L}^{\text{BF16}}_2"
